                                Translated Voting Information Available
Selected portions of this county voters’ pamphlet are available in Arabic, Simplified and Traditional
Chinese, Iu Mien, Korean, Nepali, Russian, Somali, Spanish, Tagalog, Ukrainian, and Vietnamese.
These translations can be found online at oregonvotes.gov/MultnomahVP.

                                                 05
                                             Letter to the Voter
Dear Multnomah County Voter: This is your Voters’ Pamphlet for the May 20, 2025 Special District Election.
This pamphlet is mailed to all residential households in Multnomah County and includes information about
candidates and measures from local special districts within the boundaries of the County. Local elections are
important. Please take into consideration the following information to make your vote count in this election.

The registration deadline is Tuesday, April 29, 2025. You can view your registration status and update if
necessary at OregonVotes.gov/MyVote.

    • Urban Flood Safety and Water Quality District: This is the first regular election of directors for this district.
      To implement staggered terms, the directors elected in May will draw lots after the election to determine
      which two positions will be on the ballot in May 2027 and which three positions will be on the ballot in May
      2029.
    • Six official drop sites have been impacted by library construction closures this election. These
      include: Albina, Belmont, Kenton, Rockwood, St. Johns and Woodstock libraries. If you frequent
      one of these library locations, please view our Official Ballot Drop Site Location Change Information page
      for more details on how to return your ballot at one of our new official ballot box locations. Please contact
      us if you need assistance casting your ballot.
    • For a list of official ballot drop sites in Multnomah County, please visit our website:
      MultCo.us/DropSites

Ballots will be mailed to registered voters on Wednesday, April 30, 2025. If you do not receive your ballot by
Thursday, May 8, 2025, please call 503-988-VOTE (8683) or email elections@multco.us.

To be counted, voted ballots must be received at any County elections office in Oregon or Official Ballot
Drop Site location by 8:00 p.m., Tuesday, May 20, 2025, or postmarked by Tuesday, May 20, 2025.

In addition, ahead of the election we are asking voters to Let’s Make a Plan to VOTE! Here is what you can do
to be ready for the election and ensure your vote is counted:

   1. Register to VOTE. Update your voter registration information or register to vote at OregonVotes.
      gov/MyVote. The Voter Registration Deadline is April 29. Sign up to Track Your Ballot at Multco.us/
      TrackYourBallot.
   2. Get your ballot. You will receive your ballot in the mail beginning April 30. If you have not received your
      ballot by May 8, take action and contact the elections office.
   3. VOTE your ballot. Remember to sign your ballot return envelope. Your signature is your identification. If
      you forget to sign or your signature does not match we will contact you so you can take action and we can
      count your vote.
   4. Return your ballot. Two ways to return your ballot – 1) Return by mail, your ballot envelope must be
      postmarked by Tuesday, May 20 and received within seven days to be counted. 2) Official Drop Site,
      voted ballots must be received at any County elections office in Oregon or official drop site location by
      8:00 p.m., Tuesday, May 20.
   5. Have a VOICE and make a difference. Unofficial election results will be posted after 8:00 p.m., May 20
      at MultnomahVotes.gov. Final official election results are certified by June 16, 2025.

If you have any questions, you can contact our office at 503-988-VOTE (8683).


Sincerely,

                                                                  PLEASE NOTE: Multnomah County Elections
                                                                  prints information as submitted. We do not
                                                                  correct spelling, punctuation, grammar,
                                                                  syntax, errors or inaccurate information.
Tim Scott, Multnomah County Director of Elections


                                                          06
                                      Election Definitions
Special district elections happen on the third Tuesday in May of odd years. The election on Tuesday,
May 20, 2025 is a Special District Election used to elect board members to special districts, such as
school boards, water, or fire districts.

  Cambios de ubicación de los sitios oficiales con buzones de entrega de
                           boletas electorales
Para obtener información sobre el cierre de bibliotecas del condado de Multnomah ingrese a:
MultCoLib.org/library-construction-closures. Deposite su boleta electoral en cualquier sitio
oficial de entrega de boletas electorales en Oregon a más tardar a las 8:00 p. m. el día de las
elecciones, el martes 20 de mayo de 2025. Visite nuestro sitio web: MultCo.us/DropSites para ver
una lista de los sitios oficiales de entrega de boletas electorales en el condado de Multnomah.

                                                    12
 Изменения в расположении официальных ящиков для сбора бюллетеней
Информацию о закрытии библиотек округа Multnomah для проведения строительных
работ смотрите на интернет-сайте: MultCoLib.org/library-construction-closures. Верните
избирательный бюллетень в любой официальный пункт для сбора избирательных
бюллетеней в штате Oregon не позднее 08:00 в день выборов (вторник, 20 мая 2025 года).
Пожалуйста, смотрите список официальных пунктов для сбора избирательных бюллетеней в
округе Multnomah на нашем интернет-сайте: MultCo.us/DropSites.

          Gửi lá phiếu của quý vị qua Bưu điện Hoa                      Bỏ lá phiếu tại một điểm bỏ phiếu chính

                  Các thay đổi địa điểm thùng bỏ phiếu chính thức
Để biết thông tin đóng cửa Thư viện Quận Multnomah truy cập:
MultCoLib.org/library-construction-closures. Bỏ lá phiếu tại một điểm bỏ phiếu chính thức nào
của tiểu bang Oregon trước 8 giờ tối Ngày Bầu cử hoặc trước Ngày Bầu cử, thứ Ba ngày 20
tháng 5 năm 2025. Để biết danh sách các địa điểm bỏ phiếu chính thức tại Quận Multnomah, vui
lòng truy cập trang mạng của chúng tôi: MultCo.us/DropSites.

  Зміни офіційного місцезнаходження скриньок для збору бюлетенів
Щоб отримати інформацію про закриття бібліотеки каунті Multnomah, відвідайте
вебсторінку: MultCoLib.org/library-construction-closures. Опустіть бюлетень в будь-якому
місцезнаходженні офіційних скриньок для збору бюлетенів в штаті Oregon до 20:00 не
пізніше дня виборів, вівторка, 20 травня 2025. Відвідайте наш вебсайт і перегляньте список
офіційних місцезнаходжень скриньок для збору бюлетенів в каунті Multnomah:
MultCo.us/DropSites.

                        Facts to Know about Voting and Elections
      Who can register to vote in Oregon?                                      Write-in Votes
• You must be a United States citizen,
• A resident of Oregon,
• And at least 16 years old. You will get your first
  ballot in the mail once you are at least 18 years
  old.

You must register to vote at least 21 days
before Election Day.                                         To vote for a write-in candidate who does not
                                                             appear on the ballot, completely darken the oval
            How can I register to vote?                      to the left of the line provided for the office and
• Online at OregonVotes.gov/register or                      write the full name of the candidate on that line.
• On a paper Oregon Voter Registration Card that
  can be picked up at an elections office, DMV,      • Make sure you have correctly marked your
  public library, or post office.                      choice for each contest.
• Use page 91 of this pamphlet.                      • Your official ballot may have contests printed on
                                                       both sides.
           How does vote by mail work?               • Place the ballot in the return identification
All elections in Oregon are conducted by the           envelope.
county elections office. The elections office        • Read the Voter’s Statement on the return

their ballots, and then return them to the county    • Your ballot cannot be counted if you do not
elections office in the mail or by putting them into   sign the return envelope.
an Official Ballot Drop Box.                         • Every signature is checked against the
                                                       registration signature on file. The signatures

       Vote by mail in a few simple steps:

1. Register to Vote                                          If you vote for more candidates than allowed for
                                                             an office or if you vote both Yes and No on a
2. Receive a Voter Notification Card                         measure, it is called an overvote and your vote
3. Receive a Voters’ Pamphlet                                will not count for that contest.
4. Receive Your Ballot
                                                             If you make a mistake or change your mind
5. Vote Your Ballot                                          while marking your ballot, you may correct it
6. Return Your Ballot                                        yourself. See example below.
                                                             • Make your choice obvious so that your
            How do I vote my ballot?                           intent is understood and your vote is counted
                                                               correctly.




Find the candidate or measure response (YES or
NO) of your choice. Completely darken the oval
to the left of your choice with blue or black ink. Do
not use felt tip markers.
                                                             • If you are unable to correct your ballot, you may
                                                               request a replacement ballot from the elections

        Факты, которые необходимо знать о голосовании и выборах
         Кто может зарегистрироваться для                  Голосование за кандидата, который не указан в
            голосования в штате Орегон?                             избирательном бюллетене
• Вы должны быть гражданином/гражданкой США,
• проживать в штате Орегон,
• и вам должно быть не менее 16 лет. Вы получите
  первый избирательный бюллетень по почте после
  того, как вам исполнится 18 лет.

Зарегистрироваться для голосования
необходимо не менее чем за 21 день до дня
выборов.

  избирателя штата Орегон, которую можно

  библиотеке или почтовом отделении.                   • В официальном избирательном бюллетене пункты

            Как голосовать по почте?                     сторонах.

 Несколько простых шагов голосования по почте            бюллетеня подписи должны совпадать.

                                                       или проголосуете и «за», и «против» по какому-либо

              Những điều cần biết về Bầu cử và các cuộc Tuyển cử
  Ai có thể ghi danh bầu cử ở bang Oregon?                               Viết tên vào lá phiếu
• Quý vị phải là công dân Hoa Kỳ,
• Là cư dân của bang Oregon,
• Và ít nhất 16 tuổi. Quý vị sẽ nhận được lá phiếu
  đầu tiên của mình qua thư khi ít nhất 18 tuổi.

Quý vị phải ghi danh bầu cử ít nhất 21 ngày
trước ngày bầu cử.

      Cách ghi danh bầu cử như thế nào?                    Bầu chọn bằng cách viết tên một ứng cử viên
• Trực tuyến tại oregonvotes.gov/register hoặc             không xuất hiện trên lá phiếu của quý vị, tô đậm
• Bằng giấy, thẻ ghi danh cử tri của tiểu bang             đầy đủ hình bầu dục ở bên trái của dòng in sẵn
  Oregon có thể lấy tại văn phòng bầu cử, Cơ               cho chức vụ và viết họ tên của ứng cử viên trên
  quan Quản lý cơ giới, thư viện công hoặc bưu            dòng đó.
  điện.
                                                    • Bảo đảm quý vị đánh dấu chính xác lựa chọn
  Cách thức hoạt động của bầu cử qua thư?             của mình cho mỗi tranh cử.
Tất cả các cuộc bầu cử ở bang Oregon đều do         • Lá phiếu chính thức của quý vị có thể có các
văn phòng bầu cử quận tiến hành. Văn phòng            tranh cử được in ở cả hai mặt.
bầu cử sẽ gửi trực tiếp lá phiếu bầu cử đến cử tri. • Bỏ lá phiếu vào trong phong bì nhận dạng gửi
Các cử tri hoàn thành lá phiếu của họ, và sau đó      lại.
gửi lại cho văn phòng bầu cử quận qua thư hoặc • Đọc và ký vào ô xác nhận của cử tri trên phong
bỏ vào thùng phiếu chính thức.                        bì gửi lại và ký vào ô chữ ký.
                                                    • Lá phiếu sẽ không được kiểm đếm nếu quý
                                                      vị không ký tên lên phong bì gửi lại.
 Bầu cử qua thư qua một vài bước đơn giản:          • Mọi chữ ký đều được kiểm tra dựa trên chữ ký
                                                      ghi danh trong hồ sơ lưu trữ. Chữ ký trong hồ
1. Ghi danh bầu cử                                    sơ lưu trữ phải khớp với chữ ký trên lá phiếu
                                                      mới được kiểm đếm.
2. Nhận thẻ thông báo cử tri
3. Nhận tập thông tin cho cử tri                           Nếu quý vị bầu chọn cho nhiều ứng cử viên hơn
                                                           mức cho phép cho một chức vụ hoặc nếu quý vị
4. Nhận lá phiếu bầu cử
                                                           bầu chọn cả Có và Không cho một dự luật, bầu
5. Bầu chọn trên lá phiếu                                  chọn đó sẽ được gọi là bầu chọn quá hạn và
                                                           không được tính cho phần tranh cử đó.
6. Gửi lại lá phiếu
                                                           Nếu quý vị mắc lỗi hoặc thay đổi ý định trong
                                                           khi đánh dấu lá phiếu của mình,
         Cách bầu chọn trên lá phiếu?
                                                           • Quý vị có thể tự sửa. Xem ví dụ bên dưới.
                                                           • Đánh dấu rõ ràng lựa chọn của quý vị để cho
                                                             ý định của quý vị nhìn dễ hiểu và lá phiếu được
                                                             kiểm đếm chính xác.




Tìm tên ứng cử viên hoặc câu trả lời cho dự luật
(Có hoặc Không) mà quý vị lựa chọn. Tô đầy đủ
hình bầu dục bên trái lựa chọn của quý vị bằng
mực đen hoặc xanh. Không sử dụng bút dạ
quang.

                                                           • Nếu quý vị không thể sửa lá phiếu của mình,
                                                             quý vị có thể yêu cầu một lá phiếu thay thế từ
                                                             văn phòng bầu cử.

 Macluumaadka Ay Tahay In Laga Ogaado Codaynta iyo Doorashooyinka
   Yaa isku duwaan gelin kara Doorashooyinka                           Codaynta Ka Lagu Qorayo
                      Oregon?
• Waa inaad haysataa dhalasho Maraykan ah,
• Waa inaad degan tahay Oregon,

         (This information furnished by Blake Petersen)
                                                                    Education Service District
                                                                    An Education Service District (ESD) is a regional
                                                                    education agency. ESDs work to provide the
                                                                    various school districts with a wide array of
                                                                    educational programs and services, many
                                                                    of which are too costly or limited in demand
                                                                    for a single location. Multnomah Education
                                                                    Service District is an educational cooperative
                                                                    that provides a wide variety of programs and
                                                                    services on a regional basis to school districts
                                                                    in Multnomah County and beyond. An elected
                                                                    board governs Multnomah ESD. Five members
                                                                    are chosen by zone (area in which they live), and
                                                                    two are selected from the county at-large.

Statement of Values:

I value authenticity, hard work, community listening, and
fighting on behalf of kids’ well being and for what’s right
in our education system. I stand for racial, economic, and
social justice in all aspects of education and the school
board. I believe in the autonomy of parents, while also
valuing the collective good and public health. Teachers
are one of our society’s greatest assets and leaders, and
should be treated as such.

What will you fight for?

I’m running on three main aspirations: pay teachers their
worth; incorporate climate change, social justice, arts, and
modern skills in curriculum; and invest in strong afterschool
programming.

Especially with what’s going on with disinvestments at the
federal level, we must take a stand for our children. When
we prioritize our kids, we help our democracy, economy,
and culture. I am here to talk and listen to anyone. Vote for
me and vote for our kids’ well being and success, so that
we can all thrive.

                                                                          Tuesday, May 20, 2025.

          But my work isn’t just about pipes and systems—
it’s about people. From an early age, the Winters family
welcomed me as one of their own, instilling in me a deep
appreciation for the close-knit bonds of a small community.
Later, I had the privilege of joining my wife’s family, the
Griffins of Latourell, further deepening my connection to the
traditions that make this area so special. Corbett is more
than just my home, it’s where I’m raising my family, building
lasting relationships, and investing in the future of our
community.

          As a Columbia River native fisherman, I deeply
respect the importance of our water resources. I understand
firsthand how vital these resources are, not only for
our community’s drinking water but for the health of the
ecosystems and livelihoods that depend on them.

          As a father of two, I care about keeping Corbett
financially stable and well-managed, ensuring our
community services remain effective and efficient. My
goal is to maintain and improve our infrastructure with a
responsible, common-sense approach that respects both
the needs of residents and the resources they contribute.

ARGUMENT IN OPPOSITION
         Taxpayers Association of Oregon urges
               VOTE NO ON MEASURE 26-258
  Mt. Hood Community College doesn’t need more of
                  your money!

This $136 million bond is a bad deal for taxpayers. Here’s
why:

Declining enrollment: Mt. Hood Community College has
seen dropping student numbers for years—down 20%
from pre-COVID enrollment. Why should we pay for new
buildings when there are fewer students? It doesn’t make
sense.

Too expensive: This bond will cost homeowners $60 a
year. That might not sound like much, but it adds up fast.
Over 20 years, that’s $1,200 per household—and that’s just
for the average home. Many will pay much more.

Bad timing: With prices rising and the Portland region in a
doom loop, now is not the time to raise taxes. Many families
are struggling to make ends meet.

                                         Portland School District 1J
Measure 26-259
problems they don’t want you to know about.

First, this isn’t just $1.83 billion – with interest, you’ll be
paying $3.5 billion over 32 years!

  • Almost half of every dollar goes straight to
    bondholders, not our schools.
  • This debt is already devouring our education
    budget, with interest payments skyrocketing from $1.8
    million in 2006 to $67 million today.

Second, PPS keeps breaking promises.

  • The 2020 bond included $311 million for Jefferson High
    School, but the cost ballooned to $491 million without
    a single brick being laid.
  • PPS also promised a $60 million Center for Black
    Student Excellence that was never built.

Third, even school board members say this bond is
incredibly wasteful.

  • Former board member Scott Bailey revealed that PPS
    is building high schools for 17,000 students when
    enrollment projections show only 11,000 students in ten
    years — “a huge overbuild that will waste hundreds of
    millions of dollars.” (PPS Board Meeting, 3/4/2025)

  • While still on the board, Andrew Scott admitted these
    would be “the most expensive schools ever built in
    the country.” (PPS Board Meeting, 12/3/2024)
  • Current board member Julia Brim-Edwards
    complained this bond had “the least information and
    no community stakeholder group.” (PPS Board
    Meeting, 2/4/2025)

The truth is simple: PPS wants a blank check with vague
promises.

PPS is building palaces when enrollment is dropping and
cutting classroom budgets at the same time. This isn’t
responsible stewardship of our tax dollars.

Send a clear message to PPS: We support students, but
not wasteful spending.

Vote NO on this bond and demand a better plan that
puts students before excessive construction costs.

Data shows that our emergency response system
faces significant demands, with average monthly call
volumes indicating that multiple emergency calls occur
simultaneously 50% of the time. This high call volume
reinforces the necessity of maintaining two ambulances
available 24 hours a day, ensuring a prompt response to
emergencies, regardless of when they occur.
